data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:19244 | [2025-12-30 15:02] furysk : May not want to hear it from me but I’d say salary increase by expectations are a massive bait to ruin one’s financials.  The team balance sheet at 100% is better off than at 105-120%, the team doesn’t get any additional dollars per win or sponsors to cover that.  The second issue is team expected revenues is based on a higher number than what your attendance ends up at.  So if you have a 50k stadium you might see 500k in stadium revenues instead of 400k, which on the balance sheet means the team doesn’t look to eat into facility upkeep to cover the fact that financially you might not be making all that money in reality.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:82 | [2025-06-02 19:00] derbymanager : - You can now pay off any missing money to upgrade facilities (the limit was previously at 1M and then at 2.5M). The board will still only allow upgrades up to few levels above their target (e.g. if your facilities are at 22 and target is at 16 you maybe won't be able to pay for upgrades. - There is a new spider graph for players, always visible, so <@439665894021398539> and everyone can get a better/faster overview of opponent players. The graph gives out the average value of 3 related skills and is useful to quickly make sense of what kind of player one is.  ---  *Anyway, I'll try to explain again how facilities work. Think of them as being a bank.  1. You put money into them (effectively losing it for transfers). This happens when the investment is above 100%. 2. The money in the bank gives you revenue from interest (in our case it's better training, higher salary cap, etc...). 3. You can take the money out of the bank, losing some of the interest revenue (benefits). This happens under 100% investment.  Your board aims to keep finances stable. If you don't earn money, they won't invest. If you're earning too much, they'll invest a part of it. If you're behind too much they'll invest more, if you're close to the target they'll invest less. If you want to overinvest, you have to do it yourself by earning money and manually putting it into facilities.  Allowing you to put 10M directly into the bank instead of waiting for X weeks for that to happen effectively changes only the time variable. Financially it is the same. You also get benefits from that investment but lose cash for players. And, in the end, if you can't support that level your board will lower investment below 100% and you'll get your money back (slowly over time). It's literally like moving money from one bucket (cash) to another (facilities).  Hope that helps.*

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:7212 | [2020-01-30 07:09] saltylem0n_ : **Assistant manager** takes care of squad's physical skills and helps them recover from injuries faster. He gives up to +4% per star bonus to both injury recovery and training speed for physical skills (speed, strength, work rate and set pieces).  **Technical coach** is responsible for training technical skills of your players. He increases the training speed by up to +4% per star for technical skills: tackling, accuracy, passing, technique, finishing, handling.  **Tactical coach** handles the training of tactical skills. Like others, he also increases the training speed by up to +4% per star for: ball control, anticipation, positioning, vision, off the ball and marking skills.  **Business manager** handles finances and increases the revenue from sponsors. His effort depends on your facilities level and will bring up to 60000 money per star. Use business manager if you want stable and reliable income per day.  **Brand specialist** takes care of club's brand recognition. He increases home match attendance (up to 4000 per star) and merchandise revenue (up to 40000 per star), both of which depend on fans' satisfaction. If your fans are happy and your teamwork is high, you might want to consider using brand specialists instead of business managers. While merchandise income on itself is lower than sponsors, increased income from attendance can easily offset the total revenue in brand specialist's favour.  **Scout** can bring youth players into your club and help you identify scouting targets. He increases the potential and initial power of your youths and the rate at which your club generates them, all by up to 4% per star. He will also increase the rate at which you regenerate scout reports by the same amount and help you a bit to find potential transfer targets.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:18558 | [2026-09-28 00:53] furysk : For generic advice now that sports are done for the day over here in the USA, i'd say that the following would apply to this game:  1) Finances are a lot of what makes or breaks your ability in tier 4.  The more wage budget and cash you can generate, the more margin for error or corrections you have.  the stadium in tier 4 capacity wise is .5 * stadium size * 10.  So if your stadium is 23000, the maximum you can make is 115,000.  Your team's reputation is largely responsible for the stadium filling out, and your team is at about 65k in estimated stadium revenue.  So you wont get any cash from upgrading the stadium any time soon, and would honestly need a 200's reputation to even push into the 80k-90k range.  any stadium upgrade is just losing you the cash spent to get the upgrade + the increase in Maintenance.  Sponsors on the other hand give you an additional 50k outright wage budget by adding 50k to the sponsorship deals, and if you spend an additional 30k cash you still have 20k in effective cash flow gained and 50k in wage budget gained still.  2) I would suggest while newer to place 5 players in defensive positions and 5 players in offensive positions.  Where on the pitch they are means not as much to me, just making sure enough go back to defend in a play is important, and enough go forward and generate chances equally important.  If you run a 3-4-3, in theory you should have 2 defensive midfielders, 3 defensive backs, and then two distribution/forward-thinking midfielders and a TQ/FN and a TM/CF up front (or if you want to use wings, then maybe IF's, or WF's).  As you learn more you can get away from these sorts of even measurements and experiment more, but going all turtle defense or all offense are equally problematic depending.  3) Do not go all youth, they need experienced players help to grow in tier 4.  get a solid mix of experienced and youngsters, and work to keep players rotated so they can gain experience or not lose too much conditioning.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:2198 | [2025-07-06 20:22] derbymanager : So, a quick recap of finances, again:  - Stadium revenue and match rewards are not included in daily finances as they're variable of course. You might play a home game and a cup game in one day and earn a lot, and then the next two days you can play away and earn 0. - Instead, the board uses an estimated daily stadium revenue. So, they expect you will earn 1M from today's home game, then 0 from tomorrow's away game, and so on for the whole season. Average is 500k per day and they use that for their calculations. - Based on that they will invest into facilities as much as they can without going too far into negative daily money. They also won't invest into facilities if they estimate your facilities are too high for the league.  So why are my facilities downgrading?  Because you don't have enough money to invest into them. Let's say you just sold a player and have 5M on your account. The board is downgrading your facilities by 200k per day to keep those 5M intact so you can buy players. They could, instead, keep your facilities at 100% and they wouldn't downgrade, but you would lose 200k per day and in less than a season (25 days to be exact) you would lose those 5M and couldn't improve your squad.  Since your facilities downgrade for the same price it costs to improve them you can just buy back the level you lost for cash, assuming you have cash to do so. Financially and mathematically it is exactly the same. There is no benefit if the board is investing money for you or if you do it manually yourself. It's just moving money from one bucket into another.  Hope that helps.  Also, I made a change in the morning that will invest more into facilities but you need to wait for the night update to update your finances. Tomorrow they'll invest a bit more into facilities 🤞

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:20663 | [2026-08-22 19:17] derbymanager : <@94676029846593536> I really, really appreciate people that go this deep into the game, it's soooo beneficial for me. You are right, but I think one part of the picture is missing.  There are a couple of things that work as anchor points in the game. For example, 1st most popular team gets tier 10, 50th gets tier 9. Places 2-49 are distributed proportionally to their popularity compared to the 1st and 5th.  Popularity, that determines tier, is tied to the league you're in. 1st place in d4 is IDK tier 200 or something like that. But generally speaking you can't go much higher than 200 in d4.   And tier + league define revenues, more or less. So we can treat these as static values. D4 teams get between on average X money, or at most Y money. Whatever we need. The X and Y are static since the beginning basically.  However, what's not static are players. Their values and demands are relative to their power (and a bunch of other modifiers but we can ignore these, the quality highly correlates to price). And, over time, the average quality of players increased. Training is too good (if I nerf it, people are unhappy), schools got improved (people were unhappy before), and just the general churn of shitty players makes it so that the average value of d4 team has increased significantly compared to the season 1 (or whichever older season you want to take as benchmark).  So, average player quality increased, and with that average demands have increased... but the finances are constant, as I mentioned earlier.  Giving more money to d4 teams is counter productive IMO. It leads towards inflation, while I wanted to anchor the system somehow so that I don't have to inflate every other financial thing in the game too. The fact that teams are having salary issues and struggling is because teams in d4 consist of bigger teams that have dropped down and teams that have maxed out their squads so it's hard to compete with a normal squad...

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:18 | [2026-08-23 10:55] derbymanager : As I said, raising the cap only lets bottom teams become even better. They already have average player value above 4M, of course nobody wants to buy 2-3M players...  Money goes into facilities and since it's zero sum game there it's not really a loss for any team. Basically, take United with facilities 40. They go down to d4 and it takes them 20 seasons to get to facilities 15. But they don't actually lose any money there, they just get back what they invested previously into facilities. Or in United's case, what they were generated with. I think that's the primary area I can tackle this money issue.  To put it in other words, United's facilities going from 40-39 gives them 4M back, and with those 4M they can pump back their facilities to 40. Going down has no monetary impact on facilities. But if facilities were going down and you only got back 50% or 75% of that investment, then it would actually take the money out of the game and on the right teams IMO. Sure, a season in lower division won't break you, but if you spend 5, you'll lose some value.  The newbie issue is only because d4 teams are too good. If d4 teams had players with average value of 2M then a newbie taking any team could play around with their finances to get ahead. The league would simply be weaker and it would be easier to become stronger. As long as you can earn good money in d4 (and d3, as soyaux proved), there is no pressure to actually get to d2... data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:20024 | [2023-05-30 19:15] derbymanager : Nope. The club calculates your budget based on your position and facilities... That's what you get for the season. If you end up in the negative your budget for the next season will be lower. If you're too long in the negative (several seasons) your facilities might drop as well... But the board controls how much damage you can do with the budget. Reducing your budget is basically the same as saving money. The budget will be calculated beginning of next season, so for the 2nd window you're stuck with what you have left from this window.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:4957 | [2026-08-15 14:55] derbymanager : So, to give a bit of an overview:  When you promote a player their price is lower because they haven't played any games so there is no performance multiplier on price yet. So the price you see when you promote someone from school is lower than what that player is actually worth. But if you use that price and find the player with the same price and age on the market, just compare the costs. Include the cost of school, contracts, everything until the end of their first professional contract.  For someone with facilities in the 40s total cost for 1 school year + 3 first team years is about 6M. For someone with facilities in the 20s total cost is about 3M.  A 2M player will cost you about 5M to get from the market (with 3 years of salaries and the signing fee).  Financially, even if you miss and promote a bad player, you basically can't miss with youngsters. You would have to promote <1M players that can't grow in your squad and can't be sold to actually lose money... The fact is that the youngsters were and, especially now, are too good investment.   Which brings me to the real problem and that is the money drain from the game. Every youngster that gets promoted is money that goes out of the game. Keeping them in school drains the money from the game. Buying them from the free market into the school drains the money from the game. It affects the market negatively. D4 teams don't have to trade among themselves, they can get their entry-level players from schools instead. Why would you buy 2M player when you can get him for 0?  I like the system, I like scouting youngsters, but it's problematic (and not because you can't promote all of them). So expect even more updates with restrictions and discouragement from using them 😬

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:50 | [2024-12-02 14:05] derbymanager : **New youth system** It's similar to the old one but with a shorter cycle. You have 4 players available to you that each go through the same 4-week cycle (but each is offset by 1 week): - Monday week 0: player gets generated but you only know their name and some basic info - Monday week 1: three best and three worst skills are revealed + fitness - Monday week 2: a spider chart is revealed for the player - Monday week 3: you get scout's recommendation (stars) and potential - Monday week 4 is Monday week 0 for a new player in that position and the cycle repeats  So, every week you get one new player and more info on existing players until they get removed. If you promote a player you have to wait for his regular cycle to finish before you get a new one.  **Other changes** - You get money for winning games, 100k - 400k in divisions, 500k in ass cup and national cup, and 600k in champions cup. On top of that you get end of season rewards as before, that part didn't change. - Players' salary requirements are randomised slightly so it's a bit harder to tell how good a player is by his salary. - Mentors don't get training benefit from themselves or other mentors. - Headers should only happen from right angles now. - Changed values for build up so that mids are more important for it. Let's see how that goes. - When starting a standard build up the ball placement is pulled back if there are no players nearby. So if you have DL and FL and the action starts on the left side, ball will be pulled back to DL (previously DL or MLC would move up to the ball).
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:57 | [2025-02-10 10:01] derbymanager : - Bots will now change their formation between matches to better adapt to their opponents. They switch between similar formations so that their players use familiar roles. - Added corners. After a shot, GK has a chance to catch the ball into his hands. The chance is higher with composure and agility. If he doesn't catch it, it'll be a rebound or a corner. - Added fouls. Every time a player successfully beats the defender there is a chance that the defender will stop him with a foul. The chance depends on professionalism and condition of the defender. - Roles now have a set pieces stat which defines who goes into the box (higher numbers) and who stays behind (lower numbers) on free kicks and corners. 6 players with highest numbers will go into the box. For example, limited defender will stay back at all times while complete defender will go into the box. Note that this can open up some unexpected movements afterwards... - Set pieces stat is used in combination to the "logical" stat to determine how good someone is at set pieces (e.g. direct free kick = long shot + set pieces skills). - Adjusted BU numbers for roles so that defenders prevent more build up and forwards don't contribute as much. I might adjust this throughout the season based on your feedback... - Other small match engine adjustments and fixes to animations. - You can track training of your 4 youngsters on the training screen. - Their contracts (for new ones that you recruit) will be for 3 years so plenty of time to train them. - You also get to see all of their skills as ranges immediately. Ranges get more precise slowly with time. - Potential is revealed after 1 season in the school, and all skills should be fully revealed in 2 seasons. - Youngsters now ask salaries solely based on team tier. You can't gauge their quality by their demands. - But you can now release players after a single season (that have fewer than 70 days left on contract) but you have to pay 50% of their salary to do so.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:81 | [2025-05-31 13:59] derbymanager : **Now for the new update**  - Players won't complain that they want to leave *and* want a new contract, so no double penalty. - Players will only complain if they expect about 50% (yellow) or double (red) the salary they have now, or if their demands are 25k or 50k higher than what they earn now. - Non-playable teams unintentionally had merch revenue 0 and the overall money was growing. I reduced merch revenue for all teams (and gave some of it to non-playable teams) with a goal of preventing inflation. We want (some) teams to lose money so they would have to sell their players. Right now, bots don't need to sell so the market is lame. We'll see if it gets better in the next days or not. - Removed team spirit from the game. It was clunky and didn't give much in return. Satisfaction now affects the player himself and reduces his (and only his) performance. - I'm tracking more stats now that you can see at the end of the match (might not work well retroactively). Hope this helps you decipher why you lost 🙂 - The happiness icon for each player at the end of the match doesn't take form into account anymore. It's only modifiers this player played with.

I'm still adjusting and will update the wiki at one point. - Creativity works in similar way to anticipation, as it was foretold. - Build up now depends mostly on player's tier (overall measure of quality) and experience. Creativity / Anticipation and form have a smaller impact. - Euro-cups are now held in groups of 8, each team plays 2 games against "strong" teams and two against "weak" ones in the group. After 4 games top 4 from each group go into knock out phase. - Stadium revenue is now based on attendance. Attendance is based on the two playing teams' tiers. Each competition has fixed ticket prices. Cup attendance revenue is always shared between both teams. - Roles now have "flags" what they want to do more (e.g. a role either has or doesn't have a long passes flag). The boost is now fixed, if there is a flag, this role gets a boost to decide to do that action. - Roles now have flags for headers and crosses so you can target your target man... - New training (explained below). - And other smaller tweaks...

Your expectation for next season (season 12) will be to finish 12th in D1, reducing the salary cap to 105%, but your finances are still on the D2 level so it looks like you are over the cap already...* >  > *Or the other way around, you've demoted and the percentage has gone up but the finances didn't go down (yet) so it looks like you have a huge salary cap.* >  > Thread carefully until Monday, sorry for the inconvenience...

This might have discouraged people from playing them, while playing them was needed to get better colours. So this is now changed and both form and sharpness affect the modifier separately.*

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:124 | [2026-08-22 21:13] derbymanager : **S25 update**  - **Team spirit.** As explained above. Keep in mind that transfer listing a player removes his penalty - **Satisfaction.** Similar to before except salary and contract status are merged together under contract and training and too good for the club are also merged into one complaint - team. The overall period when they won't complain after signing a contract is somewhat shorter than before and they will also be more likely to complain near the end of the contract... - **Refusing extensions.** If the player has all 3 complaints (playtime, team, contract) or he is far above your level he won't extend contract. - **Schools.** Instead of having coaches, youngsters now ask for salary equal to your facilities (which was previously the price for a coach). You can cover a small part of the salary with upfront fee if you wish though. - **Scouting youth.** You can't see the price and tier anymore, and also scouts don't show all the details in the scout section as before (stars, experience, some other bits). - **Set set piece takers.** On the player detail screen you can set some players to take set pieces. If such players are in the match squad, a random one from them will be picked. If there are none, then the game decides.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part1.txt:15576 | [2025-05-21 12:03] derbymanager : But to answer your other question, which I already explained, allowing you to decide has 3 outcomes: A - you overinvest B - you invest just the right amount C - you underinvest  Obviously it's a spectrum, it's not just clear A, then with $1 less investment it becomes B... data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:5942 | [2019-12-12 15:21] derbymanager : To quickly go through latest comments: -  On penalties fiasco: All penalties are presented, there are no hidden penalties if you use players as expected. There were no failed youths as they couldn't fail. Also, it is only expensive if you have 2-star players in B. Since price isn't so dependant on skills anymore it will now be easier (once I balance everything) to actually have Ansu Fatis in your team, but also to have Bojans... - On player's role/club fit: There will be indicators how fans perceive your players that will give you a good idea who is where. The more consistently they play - the more precise explanation you'll get. If a player has maximum club fit but you play him 10 games in 5 seasons you might get info that he shows promise... If he is a regular starter for seasons - you will get much more defined explanation of where he stands. In addition to that you'll have training effort that will indicate how well he fits with the club and average ratings to compare how well he plays. There won't be exact number because that would turn the game into chasing that one number, but a number of indicators will be shown and it will be up to you to decide...

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:9827 | [2020-05-06 18:33] derbymanager : Every staff member has a role in terms of game mechanics:  BM is the benchmark staff, he should give stable income without any risk or requirements, but he should also be outperformed by other staff members if some requirements are satisfied. BS is the first example, if you have good team spirit he should bring you a bit more money. If you, in addition, focus on buying charismatic players he should be much better. This one is quite predictable actually and you can mathematically calculate whether he's better than BM or not (which isn't something I like, I want all other functions to have a level of risk but ok). TC and TC should be beneficial if you focus on youngsters, especially if they have some traits that improve training speed, and if you support them with mentors. These require a lot of effort as you have to get the right players (both young and old) and suffer team spirit penalty while trading them (since you don't generate money). It is risky because there are many variables involved but the risk isn't all or nothing, maybe if you don't do it right you'll lose 10% or 20% of potential money you could have got from BM. AM is lesser TC or TC because he trains only 4 skills instead of 6, but in return your injuries get recovered faster. So he's a bit riskier than other coaches in terms of money earned but helps with injuries which can be significant in terms of performance and winning leagues. It's hard to put money value on that.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:9828 | [2020-05-06 18:47] derbymanager : S is a wild card, it gives you more of randomly generated value that is dependable on several parameters. You can also scout more so there is a better chance of finding the right player (who can generate money through wins or development), although this is of limited usability I'm aware.  So, the things I think about doing: - adding a penalty for having a lot of the same staff. This should release some of BM for others to use and will reduce their money generating power in bulk, while keeping it good on individual level. The penalty won't be so harsh as to prevent you from stacking, but it will try to discourage you from doing that. - making free training smaller so you'll need coaches to train players effectively as Kum suggested. Again, the change won't be huge, just a nudge in that direction. I will also increase the B team training to approx 3 stars so that you can train your youngsters a bit further before you have to promote them. - connecting the mentoring bonus from oldies with the staff so that having coaches and mentors is the optimal way to train players. Maybe youngsters will benefit from this more or something, I have to think about it still but I don't like it the way it is now...

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part2.txt:23797 | [2022-04-03 19:51] derbymanager : The article about Haaland and his salary demands that I mentioned was great. It touches on numerous topics. For example, one might think that Real covered Bale transfer (at least early years) from shirt sales. But only about $5 goes to the club from every shirt sold. Considering each shirt goes for $50-100 that's pennies. No way Manchester City would sell 200k Haaland shirts every week to cover for his 100k weekly salary. Where it does matter, though, is with sponsors. Ronaldo going to Juventus got them better sponsorship deals. If Jeep was undecided between Juventus and Inter, having Ronaldo could have made a difference. But for Haaland it's questionable. He isn't as popular as Ronaldo and Messi. He also won't be in every world cup since he doesn't play for a big country. The boost with him is probably smaller than with what you get from having Ronaldo. Haaland will grow in terms of his brand recognition, but if he doesn't fit with Pep's style and constant rotations, breaks under pressure, gets an injury or whatever, he might fall back. Neymar's brand value probably diminished when he moved from Barca to PSG. So buying Haaland at certain price/salary is not a sure thing. The formula some financial manager in City might be calculating now is that he will + be worth it only if city wins the CC twice in the next 3 seasons.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part3.txt:6699 | [2023-06-26 14:55] randykeys : You have to offer a base minimum, which is the current market price. You can't go under it.   You can raise that price by offering more in "extra fee". You do not lose this money if you don't get the player. It's just to raise the offer.   Then there's the signing fee, which is set and can't be changed.   And the salary, where there is also a base minimum up to a base maximum. You can offer anywhere between that.  So the total calculation is price + potential extra fee + signing fee + salary

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:2711 | [2025-07-31 09:56] derbymanager : The game is changing pretty fast as it's still in beta while I try to balance it all... The best approach is to simply ask here on discord and people will help you out.  From the design perspective, the goal is to reward having a balanced team, a mixture of mentors, youth prospects and everything in between. Whenever there is a perceived meta I try to nerf it so that it isn't the best (or only) way to play. Even if one approach is perhaps too good at the moment, you can count on it getting worse in the future.  In terms of how to play, my suggestion would be: - Analyse your team, make sure you have good players all around for the formation you intend to play (it's enough to just look at the stars in the squad selection screen). Don't shy away from playing players out of position, they'll learn the new position and will play well there in 5-10 games. - Generally speaking, 1 or 2 bad players in your squad won't break you. You can play a formation that you know works well and fill a position or two with 500k youngster if needed. Of course, better players usually mean better results, but that's not guaranteed and prepare that you'll get angry at match engine occasionally 😬 - If you need money you can sell one good player and buy two ok ones. For example, you sell Valle and buy two 5-8M players to fill up your squad with 3.5 star players. You have a lot of youngsters, maybe offload those you don't intend to play and buy another 5-8M player. Selling shitty players might be tough, though. - Your main goal should be to go up to 3rd division. Don't worry about player development, facilities, or anything else. I would buy older (25+) quality players that will push you up. The rewards basically double with each division so you want to get as high as possible as fast as you can.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:3322 | [2025-08-29 03:31] furysk : My Opinion, which is not that of an expert nor a high level player but as one who is currently making the effort to guide his team out of tier 4 to what appears to be a decent shot at working:   Sell the strongest 'ceiling' prospects that you have.  it is my strong opinion that in tier 4 and to some extent tier 3 that their happiness is too much to maintain. Too many veterans and your wages spiral out of control, too many youths and your results get volatile. data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:4425 | [2026-05-10 22:04] furysk : and if that is the case, they'll play closer to if not at their Values.    I'm not 100% on the next part, but i'd look at it like it was close to the following:  10M veteran Green Shape 40% of the time Blue Shape 25% of the time Yellow Shape 25% of the time Red Shape 10% of the time  10M inexperienced Green Shape 15% of the time Blue Shape 35% of the time Yellow Shape 35% of the time Red Shape 15% of the time.  If Red is zero and Green is 10M, then the inexperienced plays at 5M over the course of the season, and the veteran probably plays at 8-8.5M, the difference in contributions or games where you can use the veteran are far higher, so you gain more out of them.  Now, red doesn't mean the guy plays like he doesn't exist, it makes it easier to math.  And yellow shape can still produce a solid result as it just means the player is inconsistent shape wise.  However, doing the math with a 0-10m is much easier to communicate, so i cut corners 😄  I'd be looking at veterans at tier 4 in 3 ways  1) If they are experienced, they will play better 2) If they are experienced, they will improve happiness from players looking for a mentor 3) If they are mentors, then they eventually will retire and return between 1M and 3M to the facilities, taking the impact of their salaries out of the equation.  You should aim to have an experienced player at every rank of the formation in tier 4 is my personal opinion.  If you can afford another one or two go for it.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:4917 | [2026-08-14 18:46] derbymanager : Think of facilities as a bet in the context of youths.   Obviously, training is one of the best ways to earn money in the main squad. Buy a player, train him, sell for more.  With youngsters the price is basically 0, or close to it, even if players are worth several million. So:  - small facilities: you can get just about anyone, train them, promote them, train them more, sell them for small profit. - big facilities: you need great youngsters, train them, promote them, train them more, sell them for big profit.  So, facilities help you train them but also lock you out of shitty youngsters. You need to find those 3M+ on the market, get them to 7M to turn profit. With low facilities (which should be in low divisions) you can take a 1M player, get him to 3M and still earn money.   Facilities just increase the stakes you are playing with. In a casino you would be at the black jack table with $1000 chips, while d4 team would drop coins into a slot machine 🙂
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part4.txt:4928 | [2026-08-14 23:39] furysk : My proposal is that it should mirror stadium revenues, where each level pays a % of facilities as coaching fees.  That way the impact in dollars climbs as you climb, rather than being suffocating at a club  even with a bigger stadium than mine they only gets 65-70k revenue max from it.

data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part5.txt:5485 | [2025-03-17 20:04] poletz : I wanted to put enphasis on the fact that is useless to mix two very different things like money and facilities, but nice effort really
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part5.txt:5495 | [2025-03-17 20:10] endgamenow : The point of the table was to see how teams would perform given they maximised their potential.  Teams use money to buy players. When they buy a player, they're investing money into that player hoping they'll improve in their value (in moneyball belief, which is what I believe most people in IT4 are thinking of).  Facilities help increase the worth of said player e.g. you buy a player for 2 million, facilities increase worth of that player by 1.5 million and hey you now have a player worth 3.5 million - and then you can sell that player and gain a 1.5 million return on investment. Opportunity cost is another discussion but right now we're talking solely about player value and wanting a higher return.  As you can see, facilities have acted as a means to increase the player's value and therefore there is a direct correlation when calculating the maximum potential of a team.  As stated before, the table is designed to project the long term - "for all we know the ENLP might be more accurate the season after this one if managers don't utilise all of their resources." which is why facilities have also been counted in.
data/discord/DerbyManager/discord_DerbyManager_part5.txt:5502 | [2025-03-17 20:15] poletz : No, i didn t want to complicate things up. It was just to say that facilities should just be ignored. I mean , if u want to see who is stronger now, just look at the value (and money and maybe age).  If u want to see who ll be better tomorrow, well is not the facilities that will define it.
